國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑 ， 但仍維持高檔 ， 顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，服務業、製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐洲議會代表團於 10 月 27 日至 29 日訪問中國，料將討論稀土及綠能
技術的依賴、貿易失衡，以及歐洲企業在中國面臨的不平等待遇問題。 
⚫ 法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日
再度發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工。 
短評 
⚫ 法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革 ， 也難以快速推出安撫反對派
的妥協法案或者公共支出 。 部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延
續至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。 
⚫ 市場預期歐洲央行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場。 
日 
本 
貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月出口年增 16.7%。 
⚫ 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%。 
⚫ 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3。 
短評 
⚫ 出口維持強勁、服務需求亦持續擴張，惟中東局勢推升能源進口成本，
使貿易逆差擴大，並加重國內通膨壓力。隨成本上漲逐步由商品擴散至
服務價格，通膨黏著性可能進一步提高，為日本央行貨幣政策正常化提
供支撐；另一方面，原物料供應受限及投入成本居高不下，已開始影響
製造業動能與企業信心，後續仍須關注高成本環境對經濟成長的制約。 
中 
國 
第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利

⚫ 1-9 月累計零售額年增率持平為 1.1%。

生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%。

事件

品、家具及機械等產品，不再僅限電動車。 
⚫ 中國銀行成功發行市場首筆掛鉤 DR001 基準利率的「三農」專項金融

特定高科技產業及扶植內需來減輕貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index) 初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%， 長期通膨預期亦由3.4%升至 3.5%， 雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示 ， 此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔 ， 並維護國內產業利益 。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯 (Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免 ， 外媒亦將此次調整與11 月 3 日期中選舉連結 。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加 ， 使民眾傾向保守消費 。 若以產業區分 ， 歐元區服務業 、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

6

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁 ， 受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動 ， 出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品 ， 進一步擴散至房屋維護 、 公共運輸 、 住宿及外食等服務項目 ，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大 ， 亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐 。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

影響企業對未來景氣的信心。 
四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%， 其中製造業 、 批發零售業年增率分別達5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能 ， 抵銷建築業年減4.4%等拖累因素影響 。 整體而言 ， 第三季GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致 ；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來 ， 儘管政府10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

用相關內容。

8

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分 ， 對香港出口激增 121.1%、 對台灣 、 韓國出口增幅達96%， 對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定， 支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行 （BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

9

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息 。 央行指出 ， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，
遠高於 2%-4%的目標區間；同時核心通膨持續攀升，顯示價格上漲已由食品與能
源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖
嬰現象衝擊農業生產，進一步推高物價。此外，披索貶至歷史低點附近，增加進
口通膨風險，因此採取較激進的緊縮措施以穩定市場預期，貨幣政策重點已由支
持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出
口大增 69.2%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅成長 56.3%，抵
銷農產品出口續跌 5.7%的影響，對台灣、美國出口大增 175.2%、89.9%、對新加
坡出口增幅亦達 50.4%。9 月進口額成長 52.7%至 MYR1,814 億元，受惠出口引
申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧 、 高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展 ，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出 ，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

ab 27 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Faraday Technology
New opportunities in advanced nodes
Q326 beat on turnkey strength and non-op gains; Q426 outlook positive
Q326 EPS of NT$1.07 came in above UBS/Street expectations mainly on  non-op gains.
Q326 sales came in at NT$3.66 bn (+11% QoQ), ahead of its original guidance but in
line with its comment  during Taiwan Summit with turnkey sales up 20% QoQ given
better support from OSAT and foundries, despite NRE declining 31% QoQ. Q326 GM

decreased 2.7ppt QoQ to 42.5% on higher turnkey mix, while  OPEX grew 9% QoQ  on
salary adjustment and higher bonus accrual. Non-op gain of NT$266mn mainly includes
valuation gains on an investee company. For Q426F, it guided sales  flat  QoQ remaining
at high levels with IP and NRE up QoQ, and turnkey down QoQ. GM is guided back to
~45%, reflecting normalized ASIC economics; OPEX should decline single digit QoQ.
2027 growth outlook supported by AI, advanced packaging and 12nm node

Management guided for double-digit sales growth in 2027, with all three businesses, IP ,
NRE and turnkey, expected to grow, driven by three key catalysts: AI, advanced
packaging and new UMC 12nm project contribution. For IP , growth should be
supported by higher royalties on foundry price hikes/customer capacity expansion and
initial  contribution from 12nm node; NRE should benefit from 2nm/4nm projects
entering peak recognition, with 3-4 ASIC tape-outs targeted in H127; while MP should

see growth in both MCU and ASIC as new projects gradually ramp. AI-related mix was at
8-9% of Q326 sales, and is expected to increase throughout 2027 alongside
accelerating advanced-packaging shipments. We expect 2027E sales to grow 37% YoY.
2nm design win opens new advanced-node and product opportunities
Faraday secured a 2nm GAA special purpose  ASIC project worth several tens of millions
of US dollars in NRE, which management described as its largest-ever design win and

most advanced project to date. The project spans front-end design and IP integration,
back-end design, customized 3D DRAM and advanced packaging, and marks Faraday's
first customized 3D DRAM and hybrid-bonding project. It expects to recognize its first
NRE milestone  in H127, followed by MP targeted within 1-2 years. Beyond 2nm, Faraday
is expanding into new applications including OCS, CXL and Si-cap, leveraging its
capabilities in advanced packaging, interposer design and ASIC integration. We believe

successful execution by Faraday and its foundry partner could strengthen Faraday's
positioning in advanced-node projects and create follow-on design-win opportunities.
Valuation: Raise PT to NT$200, stay Neutral
We raise    2026E EPS (reported) from NT$3.43 to NT$3.68 on better Q326, but lower
2027/28E EPS by 4%/1% to factor in softer CE demand and higher OPEX. We raise our
PT to NT$200 from NT$190 using 24x 2027E PE (vs prior 22x). We raise our multiple as

the recent 2nm project win should boost investor confidence, but stay Neutral as the
advanced nodes (4nm/2nm) turnkey revenue recognition depends on the foundry
partner's execution. We await clearer evidence before revisiting our assumptions.

Forecast returns

Company Description
Faraday was founded in 1993, spun off from UMC's IP and semiconductor design service
department as the first IC design service company in Asia. It is an IC design and production
service provider that initially focused on specialty applications on mature nodes by partnering

with UMC and gradually upgraded to advanced nodes through working with Samsung. The
company had its IPO on the Taiwan Securities Exchange in 1999.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Faraday using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced nodes migration, further delay of
advanced packaging ramp, and growing competition in specialty projects from China IC
design service companies.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Document 130: 261029_daiwa_ASE

Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: ASE disclosed a strong set of quarterly results and guidance 
on 29 October, both beating market expectations, thanks to the strong AI 
scaling which is just getting stronger to benefit ASE’s leading-edge 
advanced packaging (LEAP) business. We raise our forecasts and 12M TP 
and reaffirm our Outperform (2) call on the stock; accumulate into any 
technical pullback.

What’s the impact: Results and guidance all beat. ASE reported 3Q26 
net profit of c.TWD27bn, beating our/consensus estimates by 25%/18% 
thanks to higher semiconductor assembly & test (SAT, or IC ATM by 
company definition) revenue which came in at TWD151bn (USD4.7bn), 7% 
above our estimate, for higher gross margin (GM) of 30.5%, 1.9pp above 
ours and topping ASE’s structural target of 24-30%. The strength was 
amplified further by lower non-operating losses, resulting in the strong 
bottom-line beat (table on p.2). Guidance was another beat thanks to the 
strong SAT business where ASE expects revenue to grow 11-13% QoQ for 
GM to top its record of 30.5% during the COVID upturn. We raise our 2026-
28E EPS by 14-24% assuming its SAT GM to reach 33% through 2027.

Strong AI gets stronger. Discussions centred on ASE’s LEAP business, 
where management further hiked revenue target this year beyond USD4bn 
(USD1.6bn in 2025), after 3 hikes YTD, thanks to strong demand for its on-
substrate (oS) packaging and initial ramp of its full-process solutions. With 
addition of new co-packaged optics (CPO) solutions, ASE expects the 
LEAP revenue to double YoY in 2027. As LEAP is margin-accretive, ASE 
expects the SAT GM to continue improving beyond 4Q26, making the 
ceiling of its structural GM target now as the floor to kick off 2027 for a 
strong start. Given management’s emphasis on “friendly pricing”, we expect 
likely continued cost passthrough across the supply chain to add to GM 
upside. Working hard to narrow the supply/demand gap, ASE further raised 
this year’s capex to USD11.5bn (from USD10.5bn), reinforcing our view 
that AI is scaling strongly to accelerate the chip industry growth.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M TP 
for ASE to TWD710 (previous: TWD550), based on our revised ROE-
adjusted PBR target of 5.8x (previous: 5.2x) 2027E BVPS due to a higher 
ROE trajectory. Our revised TP translates into 24x 4-quarter forward PER, 
which we see as justifying the rich PBR target given its 2026-28E EPS 
CAGR of 25%. We reiterate our Outperform (2) call with key downside risks 
of any “silicon inflation” and geopolitical overhang leading to AI capex cut.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 5-10% above the consensus as we 
believe the street has yet to factor in its stronger LEAP business.

29 October 2026

Entering an even stronger earnings cycle (III)

➢ 3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance all beat expectations 
➢ Strong AI scaling is getting even stronger to benefit LEAP 
➢ Reaffirm Outperform (2) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD710

Company profile 
Established in April 2018, ASE Industrial Holding Co (HoldCo) is a holding company which privatized 
ASE and SPIL fully on 30 April 2018 through equity and cash swap, respectively, and listed on the same 
day with senior management assigned mainly by ASE. HoldCo has become the strong No.1 OSAT 
maker in the world, offering a comprehensive product portfolio to its customers from wirebonding, flip-
chip & bumping, system-in-package (SiP) to broad-based electronics manufacturing services (EMS).

### Page 5

5

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 5 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
ASE’s Board of Directors consists of nine members with diverse professional backgrounds.
Three of the directors are independent, representing 33 percent of the board. The Chairman also
serves as the Chief Executive Officer, resulting in unified leadership and centralized decision-
making. To maintain effective governance and oversight under this structure, ASE relies on its
independent directors and established board processes. The board currently includes one
female director, and the company continues to pursue long-term goals related to board diversity
and balanced representation. ASE has an Audit Committee, Compensation Committee, Risk
Management Committee, and Chief Government Officer to conduct effective supervision.
Capital management 3
In the past 5 years, ASE paid over 50% or nearly 50% of its earnings in dividends. However, the
dividend trend was volatile in dollar and payout ratio terms. We forecast its dividend payout ratio
to average over 50% in the next 2 years.
Related party &
transaction 2
ASE undertakes related-party transactions with its subsidiaries on sales and purchases. The
total related-party transaction sales/purchase amounts were less than 10% of its total
sales/purchases, and the transaction terms with related parties were normal compared with
those with other customers/suppliers. Thus, we do not think related-party transactions have a
meaningful impact on overall operations.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
The supply of raw materials has the most direct impact on ASE's operations and manufacturing.
To lower overall supply-chain risks, ASE has expanded the scope of sustainability risk
management to non-tier-1 suppliers. ASE currently has over 600 non-tier-1 suppliers.
ASE requires its suppliers to comply with its Supplier Code of Conduct, which aims to ensure
that suppliers conduct business in a manner that meets labour, health & safety, environment,
business ethics & management, and various corporate compliance standards.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
ASE has adopted 3 water-use strategies of reduce, reuse and recycle. Through its water
management measures, it reduced water consumption density (water consumption/revenue) by
43% in 2024 vs. that of 2015. ASE is committed to increasing water reuse at its facilities in order
to improve water efficiency.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
ASE is committed to improving waste recycling and reuse through the 5 key elements of a
circular economy of: direct recycling, reuse, off-site regeneration, renewable materials, and
supply and rental. The recycling rate of general waste was 90% in 2024 and its hazardous waste
density declined by 53% in 2024 vs. that of 2015.
E GHG emissions 1
ASE is committed to achieving net zero emissions by advancing low carbon energy adoption,
developing smart grid technologies, and strengthening high tech energy management systems.
As of 2024, 19% of total manufacturing energy consumption comes from renewable sources,
88% of ASE facilities use renewable energy, and 10 sites have achieved RE100 certification.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

研 究  報 告                
上品(4770.TT) 
產業：化學工業 
AI 需求帶動訂單攀升，擴產與高毛利產品推升獲利成⻑  
 投資評價與建議 
考量AI驅動全球半導體擴產 ， 帶動氟素設備 、 化學品槽⾞與建廠⼯程訂單成⻑ ，
且低毛利舊單消化、高毛利產品放量及兩岸產能擴充推升毛利率，參考同業日
本AGC、Daikin PE區間13-21倍，基於2027年EPS給予17倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
AI需求推升訂單動能 ：3Q26單季營收季增22.5%至16.0億元 ， 受惠AI帶動國內
晶圓廠客戶持續建廠，推升氟素內襯設備與管道系統的需求，年初在手訂單38
億元陸續轉為實質營收貢獻，且低毛利訂單已消化完畢，預期在高毛利訂單出
貨下，推估毛利率進⼀步提升1.38ppts至37%，稅後EPS 4.46元，2026全年營
收年增18%至55.8億元，毛利率提升3.6ppts至36.58%，稅後EPS 14.45元。 
擴產與高階新品推升獲利成⻑ ：2027年受惠兩岸產能擴充及高單價槽⾞ 、 建廠
設備進入交貨高峰 ， 2026年槽⾞接單量已超越去年的2倍突破1000台 ， 台灣廠
月產能將由80台提升至140台，並規劃進⼀步擴至170台，中國嘉興廠亦將提
升產能20%，預計自1Q27起挹注營收，產品方面，高溫環境用離型膜、高純
度配件及過濾產品將隨客戶認證逐步放量 ， 帶動高毛利產品比重提升 ， PTFE低
介電材料則仍處配方測試階段，具中⻑期成⻑潛⼒，受惠在地化供應、⼯程整
合服務及產能利用率提升 ， 預估2027年營收年增25%至69.7億元 ， 毛利率提升
2.92ppts至39.5% ，費用率因非大幅建廠 ， 維持在11.8% ， 稅後EPS 19.84元 。  
 投資風險 
客戶擴產進度延遲、高階新品開發進度不如預期、原料成本壓抑獲利表現。

| 公司簡介  
主要進口上游高階氟素樹脂 ， 如 PFA 、 PTFE 原料 ， 並自行加⼯為半成品的板材與管材 ， 以及製造終端高階等設備 ，
核心產品包括內襯設備(桶槽、反應釜)、內襯管道與管件、化學品運輸槽⾞以及⼯程服務等，這些產品主要應用於
先進半導體製程，包含 2nm 以下，與高純度電子級化學品，如硫酸、氫氟酸、光阻劑等的生產、儲存、輸送與廢
液回收系統，由於氟素材料具有極佳的耐酸鹼腐蝕與抗污染特性，設備能將金屬離子控制在 PPT 等級以下、顆粒
控制在 0.02 以下，為晶圓代⼯與特化廠維持極高良率與穩定品質的關鍵所在。 
截至 2Q26 產品別營收，內襯設備佔比 68.3%，為公司最核心的營收來源，包含特化廠與晶圓廠建廠所需的整套
重化線純化槽、儲罐、反應釜及槽⾞等設備，其他材料與半成品 16.7%，主要為第⼀階段加⼯的氟素板材與管材
半成品，多數留作自用，部分出口給海外(日本、歐洲)缺乏加⼯業者的獨立市場，內襯管道與管件 9.6%，用於連
結各機台設備與廠區內的化學品輸送管線系統，⼯程業務 5.4%，協助電子化學品廠內部的襯裡與管道施⼯，受惠
於市場技術人⼒缺乏，該業務毛利率較佳。 
公司在台灣彰濱設有主要生產基地與研發中心，並於中國浙江嘉興設有⼯廠及美國亞利桑那州設有子公司，截至
2Q26 地區別營收，台灣佔比 49.3%，為目前成⻑動能最強的區域，受惠於晶圓代⼯龍頭與特化廠強勁建廠需求，
台灣廠同時負責供應美洲及全球非中國市場、中國 44.8%，由中國廠獨立生產運作，專門供應中國內需半導體與
特化廠擴建需求、美國 3.4%，因當地晶圓廠建廠審核較慢且缺乏專業⼯程技術人⼒，推進時程較⻑，目前營收佔
比偏低、歐洲 1.5%、其他 1%。 
圖表、上品主要產品及產業概況

| 營 運 概 況 
擺脫低谷舊單稀釋，產品組合高階化使毛利提升：1H26 合併營收 23.6 億元，YoY+4%，其中半導體產業佔營收
高達 91.3%，主要受惠全球晶圓代⼯龍頭與記憶體大廠積極擴產，現有特化廠產能無法滿足晶圓廠開出的需求，
帶動建廠與特化重化線設備強勁拉貨 ， 其餘為傳統石化產業占 6.4% ， 做為維持基礎⼯業需求的穩定貢獻 ， LCD 面
板與太陽能佔 1.2%，其他佔 1.1%，毛利率年減 1.7ppts 至 35.3%，下滑主因在於認列的營收中，相當大部分來
自 2025 年半導體與特化產業處於景氣低谷期時所接的舊訂單，因當時因市場需求薄弱、競爭較為激烈，產品平

### Page 3

3 
均毛利率較差且接單總量偏低，進而稀釋 1H26 整體毛利表現，其中 2Q26 單季毛利率季增 1.7ppts 至 35.6%，
顯示高毛利的訂單已逐漸回升，1H26 費用年增 25%至 3.25 億元，其中 2Q26 單季費用季增 4%至 1.65 億元，
主因包含公司為維持先進製程氟素設備的技術領先，研發支出佔營收比重較去年同期增加 1ppts，另外公司依據
會計準則提列了預期信用風險損失評估，約占費用的 2%，若扣除信用風險損失評估，實質營業費用率仍穩定維持
在 11-12%區間 ， 1H26 業外 0.44 億元 ， 主要來自利息收入增加 ， 以及美元與人⺠幣升值所帶來的匯兌收益 ， 2Q26
所得稅因集中認列未分配盈餘加徵 5%稅賦，有效稅率較 1Q26 拉升 7ppts 至 21.6%，稅後 EPS 5.63 元，3Q26
單季營收季增 22.5%至 16.0 億元，受惠於國內客戶持續建廠，年初在手訂單 38 億元轉為實質營收貢獻，且低毛
利訂單已消化完畢，推估毛利率進⼀步提升 1.38ppts 至 37.0%，稅後 EPS 4.46 元。

受惠全球 AI 市場需求與 ESG 回收政策，帶動氟素相關產品需求：截至八月底在手訂單已突破 75 億元，公司預估
2026 全年新接訂單將年增 91%，由於 AI 需求推升全球半導體晶圓廠展開大規模擴產，目前僅晶圓代⼯龍頭在台
灣與海外同時進行興建的晶圓廠就多達約 20 座，隨著客戶現場建廠與設備拉貨排程推進，主要電子級化學品相關
製造商均必須進行大規模擴產與建廠，另配合政府政策、ESG 環保法規，中鼎(9933)等大型⼯程統包商均跨入回
收領域，直接推升氟素內襯設備與管道系統的需求，化學品運輸槽⾞因應晶圓廠大量使用化學品，預估接單量將
突破 1,000 台，相較去年 600 台是翻倍成⻑，目前槽⾞設備的訂單交期已往後推三年陸續交貨，客戶嚴格把關進
程，確保原料與槽⾞交期不致拖累建廠進度，由於產出的大型槽體與設備體積龐大，產品製造完成入庫後，必須
待客戶建廠空間準備就緒並通知拉貨才能認列營收，因此單季成⻑趨勢比單月數據更為準確，預估 2026 全年營
收年增 18%至 55.8 億元，在成本與毛利率方面，上游氟素樹脂原料，如大金(Daikin)、科慕(Chemours)，全球出
現嚴重供不應求，交期⻑達 8 個月以上，供需缺口約在 5-10%，且面臨 PFAS 環保審查與附加稅費增加的壓⼒，
但公司為原料廠的⼀線直接採購客戶，享有優先供料保障，並具備從原始樹脂顆粒自製半成品(板材/管材)到終端
設備的能⼒，另外由於公司產品設備外殼高達 90%為白鐵(不鏽鋼)，透過高市佔率優勢，能將每⼀筆新接訂單動
態重新計價轉嫁給客戶來降低成本壓⼒，預期在高毛利的產品訂單陸續開出，使整體毛利率回升 3.6ppts 至
36.58%，稅前盈餘成⻑ 40%至 14.4 億元，稅後 EPS 14.45 元。

產能擴充，持續投入高階新產品開發：2027 年預期營收受惠高能見度槽⾞與建廠設備大批量交付認列，高單價
與高毛利設備將於明年進入出貨高峰，另外新擴充的產能部分，以槽⾞為單位，中國嘉興廠進行 20%的產能提
升、動線優化與組裝委外調整，台灣廠已從每月 80 台提升至 140 台，皆於 1Q27 開始貢獻，台灣廠預計明年規
劃再提升 20%至 170 台，有效紓解目前產能滿載的壓⼒，支撐明年營收大幅成⻑。 
公司持續投入新產品研發，其中離型膜應用於晶圓廠高溫區環境的關鍵新產品，已開始小量出貨，預計於 2026
年底完成送樣，明年貢獻營收，高純度配件與包材，包含關閉時僅留 90% 殘留的射出件接頭、100L ⼀體成型
槽⾞杯、新型取樣器、濾芯過濾產品等，隨著客戶認證通過，高毛利產品出貨比重將在明年進⼀步拉高，⼯程業
務高毛利貢獻持續挹注，主要因應特化廠建廠與回收系統需求，化學廠內部的管道與襯裡⼯程訂單持續增加，在
⼯程人⼒極度缺乏的市場環境下，公司內部⼯程團隊提供整合性服務，使⼯程業務維持高毛利率表現，持續挹注
明年獲利，由於同業競爭對手大多採取保守態度不敢大幅投資，公司在地化供應優勢與積極擴產與技術升級策
略，預估市佔率將持續攀升，有助於毛利率表現。 
另因應 AI 與高頻高速傳輸技術的突破，如 PTFE/氟素樹脂與銅箔結合的低介電常數與低介電損耗(Low Dk/Df)特
性需求迫切，公司與特定下游廠商合作，進行高黏性 PTFE 氟素基板材料與金屬銅箔結合的開發與測試，由於技
術非常高，氟素樹脂化學惰性極強，提升其高黏著性並與銅箔緊密貼合，同時保持極佳的高頻電性，屬於材料科
學上的高難度突破，目前開發進度尚未正式通過客戶驗證，仍處於配方調整測試階段，屬中⻑期高毛利產品線。

### Page 4

4 
預估 2027 全年成⻑ 25%至 69.7 億元，受惠兩岸新產能開出、高毛利槽⾞與高階新產品比重提升，毛利率提升
2.92ppts 至 39.5%，費用率因公司擴充產能主要仰賴人員技術培訓、廠區動線優化與前後段⼯序，非大幅興建
廠房，因此未來不會產生龐大折舊費用來墊高費用率，隨營業收入規模擴大而稀釋，預期維持在 11.8%，營益率
提升 3.6ppts 至 27.7%，稅前盈餘年成⻑ 40.8%至 20.2 億元，稅後 EPS 19.84 元。

1 
6770 力積電(Powerchip)

報告日期： 2026/10/22 研 究 員：羅  金 鳳 (Sunny Lo)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 在記憶體及邏輯產品價格季增 20% 的帶動下，使整體毛
利率跳升 19 個百分點 ， 來到47.69% ，單 季 稅 後EPS 1.38 元，
優於預期。  
 4Q26 將反映晶圓投片及產出的時間差，使出貨量及 ASP 將再
增加，帶動營收、毛利率雙位升。  
 AI 帶動矽電容需求大爆發，目前力積電產能僅 1 萬片 /月，但
2027 年需求將達 3～5 萬片 / 月。

一. 反映投片與產出落差， 4Q26 營收、毛利率雙升  
力積電 3Q26 晶圓出貨量 41.6 萬片， QoQ-1.44% ，主因為銅鑼廠
機器搬遷所致，且 3D AI Foundry 營收占比也因遷廠而由 2Q26
的 5.4% 下滑到 1.4%， 但因記憶體及邏輯產品價格持續上漲 ，ASP
季增 20% ，使力積電 3Q26 整體營收達 212.78 億元，
QoQ+23.07% ，YoY+79.71% ，在價格上漲及產能利用率提升的帶
動下，整體毛利率由 2Q26 的 28.28% 大幅上升至 47.69% ，稅後
EPS 1.38 元，優於預期。

由於力積電投片與產出的落差 30%( 邏輯遞延 2～3 個月， DRAM
遞延 4 個月 )，因此， 4Q26 的晶圓出貨及 ASP 將比 3Q26 更高，
加上 ， 銅鑼廠的機器設備已完成搬遷到新竹廠 ，Interposer 及 WoW
的產能將在 11～12 月逐步恢復生產，其中 Interposer 將於 2027
年達到滿載。綜上，我們預估力積電 4Q26 營收 234.3 億元，
QoQ+10.11% ，YoY+87.51% ，毛利率進一步提升至 51.81% ，單
季稅後 EPS 1.64 元，合計 2026 年營收 755.71 億元，
YoY+61.72% ，在記憶體價格大漲的帶動下，毛利率由 2025 年的
-3.29% 跳升至 37.8% ，稅後 EPS 6.58 元(含售廠給美光 )。

二. 2027 年矽電容需求大爆發  
力積電營運轉型將陸續展現成果，美光授權生產的 DRAM 的 1P
製程，將於 2026 完成 PDK ，2Q27 試產， 2028 年有機會貢獻全
年營收； HBM 的後段製程代工 PWF ，由美光提供設備， 2026 年
試產， 4Q27 量產， 2028 年貢獻全年營收； 3D AI Foundry 方面，
堆疊技術將由 Micro bump 進入到 Hybrid bonding ，以及
Interposer 的放量生產 ， 在外溢效果持續下 ，2027 年產能將滿載；
Wafer on Wafer 方面，在銅鑼廠設千完全搬遷至新竹廠後，目前

公司研究報告

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

2 
已小量試產， 1H27 將正式量產。由於建廠及買購設備需大額資本
支出，風險相對較高，因此， PWF 由美光提供設備，未來的 WoW
也將會採取輕資產的模式，降低營運風險，不過，因大廠均將產
能移去生產高階 DRAM ，38nm 、25nm 、20nm 這些傳統 DRAM
製程未來全球將只剩力積電生產，預估此一需求將長達 10～15
年。

此外 ， 因AI 晶片的高功耗及高速傳輸需求 ， 為提升電源傳輸效率 、
降低電阻、去雜訊等問題，將帶動 IPD 需求大爆發，力積電目前
以現有 DRAM 的 38nm、25nm 製程做生產 IPD，恰好目前 DRAM
產能供不應求的情況下 ，IPD 的產能只能配置約 1 萬片 /月，而2027
年全球 IPD 的需求將達 3～5 萬片 /月，且能見度已延伸至 2028
年，顯見需求遠超過力積電所能提供，因此，力積電已在尋找建
廠用地，且將聯合策略客戶共同投資新產能。

三. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 8.63 元 
AI 進入到推理後對記憶體已需求量更甚以往，但因全球記憶體產
能嚴重供不應求的情況下， GUP 大廠將 12 層 HBM 降低到 8 層，
顯見記憶體緊俏情況嚴峻，也使記憶體價格易漲難跌，預估 2027
年記憶體價格仍至少維持在高點，加上成熟製程處在有利的價格
環境， 2026 年漲價雙位數後， 2027 年將再漲 10～20% ，因此，
我們預估力積電 2027 年營收 1223.38 億元， YoY+61.89% ，毛利
率 49.89% ，稅後 EPS 8.63 元。

四. 投資建議為買進，目標價 95 元 
受惠 AI 帶動的記憶體漲價潮持續， 2026 年力積電將呈現轉虧為
盈，且 2026 及 2027 年獲利都將大幅成長，加上銅鑼廠售予美光
且變成長期的代工合作夥伴關係，有利力積電長期營運穩定性，
也解決力積電資產負債表壓力，因此，投資建議為買進，目標價
95 元(11X2027 年稅後 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
力晶集團 05/2019 完成企業重組 ， 由旗下的力晶科技3 座 12 吋晶
圓廠及相關營業、資產，讓與力晶積成電子製造股份有限公司 (簡
稱“力積電” (6770)) 。力積電為聚焦專業晶圓代工、產業定位明
確，目前擁有 2 座 8 吋及 3 座 12 吋晶圓廠，將針對先進記憶體、
客製化邏輯積體電路與分離式元件的三大晶圓代工服務主軸，持
續 Open Foundry 營運模式，從晶片設計、製造服務，到設備、

產能分享，根據不同客戶的屬性和需求，共同建立緊密、彈性的
合作機制，且著眼半導體 3D 堆疊產業大趨勢，力積電與國際知名
大客戶密切配合開發 3D 堆疊封裝，將成為公司長線另一成長動
能。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1、佈局 3D IC 為公司未來運重要利基 
力積電有部份產品屬於記憶體產品，營運雖然將隨著市場波動，
受產業景氣循環大，然力積電善用其技術優勢，將其記憶體產品
與國際知名大廠合作，延伸至 3D 堆疊封裝技術，僅需利用舊製程
即可將晶片效能數倍提升，其以 55nm 的邏輯 IC 堆疊 38nm 的
DRAM 製程，封裝後的晶片用於挖礦機，效能較現有的解決方案
大幅提升 9 倍。由 於 透 過3D 堆疊可使晶片設計效率提高， 提升晶
片整體效能，減少晶片尺寸，加快晶片內部元件溝通速度，為半
導體先進封裝未來重要發展趨勢，且因先進封裝技術成本不斷提
升，客戶為考量使用的成本效益，未來將有更多廠商使用 3D 堆疊
的解決方案取代先進製程技術 ， 使得3D 堆疊技術長線發展具極大
潛力，力積電長線著眼於此一產業大趨勢，將積極深化此一佈局，
為公司長線營運提供良好動能。

2、四大營運方向引領公司未來成長動能 
力積電在經過 2019 年 3 座 8 吋廠及 2 座 12 吋廠的整併，加上邏
輯技術逐漸步上軌道後，使其在 40nm ～0.11um 的技術於全球站
穩腳步，具有指標地位。公司除將以此為基礎拓展多元應用，在
新技術方面亦不斷投入研發，展望未來，擬定四大方向引領公司
成長動能： 1、發展更先進的技術 --- 第四代半導體氧化銦鎵鋅
(IGZO) ，因 IGZO 的材料特性，其超低功耗的性能，僅為目前耗
電的 1/20， 及顯示器解析度能超過5,000DDIP ， 有助應用於AR/VR
的裝置有較佳續航力及超高解析度，使用者將完全沈浸於元宇宙
世界。 2、3D-Interchip 堆疊技術：與晶圓代工大廠台積電合作，
將記憶體與邏輯 IC 進行 3D 堆疊 ， 使晶片具更小體積 、 更低功耗，
更低成本，及開發更有效率。 3D 堆疊的 Wafer on Wafer 目前以
供應挖礦機需求為主，未來將專注於 AI 龐大需求。 3、Interchip ：
將邏輯元件直接放入記憶體元件，以提高記憶體效能，適用於
IoT 、AI 及邊緣運算。 4、車用電子晶片：因應節能減碳的趨勢，
電動車 / 新能源車將快速發展，公司將致力推進第三代半導體
GaN、SiC 技術製程，應用於 PMIC 。由於 PMIC 為車汽應用的重
要零組件之一，且 PMIC 的製造非用最先進的半導體製程，力積
電目前的製程範圍恰好符合最佳生產製程的節點，而電動車的興
起將帶動 PMIC 的應用大幅成長，加上 AI 趨勢確立下，對 PMIC
有更高需求，為公司未來重要佈局方向及營運重要成長動能。

4 
叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.64 元 
由於力積電投片與產出的落差 30%( 邏輯遞延 2～3 個月， DRAM
遞延 4 個月 )，因此， 4Q26 的晶圓出貨及 ASP 將比 3Q26 更高，
加上銅鑼廠的機器設備已完成搬遷到新竹廠， Interposer 及 WoW
的產能將在 11～12 月逐步恢復生產，其中 Interposer 將於 2027
年達到滿載。綜上，我們預估力積電 4Q26 營收 234.3 億元，
QoQ+10.11% ，YoY+87.51% ，毛利率進一步提升至 51.81% ，單
季稅後 EPS 1.64 元。

2、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 8.63 
AI 進入到推理後對記憶體已需求量更甚以往，但因全球記憶體產
能嚴重供不應求的情況下， GUP 大廠將 12 層 HBM 降低到 8 層，
顯見記憶體緊俏情況嚴峻，也使記憶體價格易漲難跌，預估 2027
年記憶體價格仍至少維持在高點，加上成熟製程處在有利的價格
環境， 2026 年漲價雙位數後， 2027 年將再漲 10～20% ，因此，
我們預估力積電 2027 年營收 1223.38 億元， YoY+61.89% ，毛利
率 49.89% ，稅後 EPS 8.63 元。

2026.09.21   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속 Company Brief

3Q26 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 영업이익 시장 기대치 상회 전망 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 8.8조원(+45% YoY, + 16% QoQ), 영업이익 3,690억원(-
39% YoY, +226% QoQ)을 기록하며 원/달러 환율 하락으로 매출은 시장 기대치를 
소폭 하회하겠으나 영업이익은 상회할 것으로 전망된다. AMPC 는 미국 ESS 생산량 
증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 힘입어 전분기 대비 약 57% 증가한 3,790억원으로 
추정된다. 미국 상호관세 위법 판결에 따른 관세 환급금이나 완성차 업체들의 최소 
구매 물량 미달 보상금 반영 여부에 따라 추가적인 이익 개선 가능성도 존재한다. ESS 
부문은 전분기에서 이연된 물량의 출하가 본격화되면서 3Q26 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
38% 증가할 것으로 예상된다. 특히 미국 ESS 생산라인의 가동 안정화와 AMPC 수혜 
확대에 힘입어 수익성도 점진적으로 개선될 것으로 보인다. 자동차전지 부문도 
3Q26부터 회복세가 강화될 것으로 예상된다. 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 
재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 큰 폭으로 증가하고, 고정비 부담 완화로 적자폭도 
뚜렷하게 축소될 전망이다. 소형전지 부문은 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 
안정적인 출하 증가로 매출 회복과 수익성 방어가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 
4Q26 대규모 ESS 신규 수주 기대 
전세계 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 힘입어 빠르게 
성장하고 있다. 시장조사기관 SNE리서치에 따르면 1H26 전세계 ESS 배터리 
출하량은 461GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 71% 증가했다. 이 중 북미 출하량은 
76GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 83% 증가했으며, 분기별로는 1Q26 35GWh(+91% 
YoY), 2Q26 41GWh(+76% YoY) 를 기록했다. 통상 하반기에 출하가 집중되는 
계절성을 감안하면 올해 북미 ESS 배터리 출하량은 전년 대비 약 70% 증가한 
170~180GWh에 이를 것으로 전망된다. 연초 동사가 예상한 올해 북미 ESS 수요 
성장률 10% 초반 수준을 크게 상회할 가능성이 높은 상황이다. 올해 동사의 ESS 
신규 수주 목표는 약 90GWh이며, 현재까지 약 30GWh의 수주를 확정한 것으로 
파악된다. 이에 시장 일각에서는 연간 수주 목표 달성 여부에 대한 우려가 제기되고 
있다. 그러나 당사가 파악한 바에 따르면 대규모 ESS 신규 수주가 4Q26에 집중될 
것으로 예상된다. 특히 북미 ESS 수요의 가파른 증가세와 투자세액공제(48E)에 
연계된 PFE 공급망 규제 시행은 동사의 수주 확보에 우호적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 향후 국내 배터리 셀 업체들의 
북미 ESS 신규 수주가 지속적으로 확대될 전망이다. 미국 ESS 시장에서는 신재생 
에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 따른 수요 증가, 중국 공급망에 대한 규제 
강화가 맞물리면서 국내 업체들에 우호적인 사업 환경이 조성되고 있다. 동사 역시 
ESS 중심의 생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화될 경우 매출 성장과 함께 고정비 
부담 완화, AMPC 수혜 확대에 따른 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 이를 바탕으로 동사의 
영업이익은 2026년 약 0.8조원에서 2028년 약 5.1조원으로 가파르게 증가할 것으로 
전망된다. ESS의 역할이 신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AIDC의 안정적인 
전력 공급을 지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 
이에 따라 동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 
산업의 성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다고 판단된다. 업종 내에서 실적 
개선 가시성과 정책 수혜가 뚜렷한 동사에 대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다.

Document 245: AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年10月 29日

1/8 
AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか 
～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ 日銀は 2026年 10月の金融システムレポートで、人工知能（AI）関連投資への期待が縮小し
た場合の金融安定上のリスクを分析した。本稿では同レポートに基づき日本へのリスクの波
及経路を整理したうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。 
○ 米国の巨大IT企業によるAI関連投資が急拡大している。これらの企業は従来の自己資金に
加え、社債発行などによる外部資金調達を増やしている。データセンター事業では、長期リ
ース契約を裏付けとした銀行融資や、プライベートファンドによる投融資が広がっている。
AI関連の旺盛な資金需要は経済成長につながる一方、特定分野へのリスクの集中と表裏一体
の関係にある。 
○ 日本への影響の波及経路として、銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つが考えられる。日本
の大手行は海外のデータセンター事業者やファンドへの融資を増やしている。また、AI関連
株の下落をきっかけにヘッジファンドが投資ポジションを縮小すれば、日本の株式市場だけ
でなく、国債市場にも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。AI関連投資の減速によるアジア経済の悪化
も、日本の金融機関の信用コスト増加につながり得る。 
○ 日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小とノンバンクによるショックの増幅
を想定しても、日本の金融機関は相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関連
企業の間では出資と将来の売上高の約束が結びついた循環的な取引がみられ、その影響はハ
イパースケーラーを通じて日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資にも間接的に及び得る。
取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要だ。

1. はじめに 
日銀は10月23日、金融システムレポートを公表した。IMFが先に公表した国際金融安定性報告
書（GFSR）と同様、金融安定上のリスク要因として人工知能（AI）に焦点を当てた。AI関連企業
の成長期待を背景に、世界の株式市場では株価上昇が続き、データセンターなどへの設備投資も
拡大している。一方、投資規模が膨らむにつれて、巨額の投資に見合った収益が実現するかどう
かが金融市場の大きな関心事となっている。

### Page 2

2/8 
今回、注目したいのはAI関連投資と海外のノンバンク金融仲介機関（NBFI）の動向だ。AI関連
投資への期待が縮小した場合、海外ノンバンクの資産売却などを通じてショックが増幅され、日
本の金融市場にも波及する可能性を日銀は指摘している。本稿では、日銀の分析をもとに、AI関
連投資を支える資金調達の変化と、海外ノンバンクを通じた日本へのリスクの波及経路を整理し
たうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。

2. 拡大するAI投資とノンバンクの役割 
AI関連投資は近年、大幅に増えている。日銀の図表によると、米国の巨大IT企業（ハイパース
ケーラー）5社の設備投資額は、2022年頃の四半期約400億ドルから2026年 4～6月期には約
1,800億ドルと4倍以上に増加した（注1） 。これらの企業は従来、潤沢な営業キャッシュフロー
をもとにデータセンター投資を行ってきた。ところが、足元では社債発行などによる外部資金調
達を増やすようになった。投資額の大きいデータセンターを例に見ていこう。 
ハイパースケーラーでは、自社でデータセンターを建設するだけでなく、外部事業者と長期リ
ース契約を結んで利用権を確保する動きも目立っている。5社の未開始のリース契約額は2026年
4～6月期に約1.1兆ドルに達した（資料1、注1）。

資料 1 米ハイパースケーラー5社の設備投資と資金調達

（注）集計対象はAlphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracleの 5社。①の「ネット借入・社債発行」と「ネット株式発行」は、プラス
が資金調達。直近は 2026年 4～6月期。数値は日本銀行の図表から筆者が読み取った概数。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-8（原資料はLSEG Workspace、SEC）をもとに第一ライフ資
産運用経済研究所作成

データセンターを建設・保有する事業者は、ハイパースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付け
に、銀行などから建設資金を調達している。銀行が融資を組成し、その貸出債権をプライベート
ファンドが購入する事例や、ファンドがデータセンター事業に直接出資する事例もある。こうし
て、AI関連投資を支える資金の出し手として、銀行に加えてプライベートファンドなどのNBFIの

### Page 3

3/8 
存在感が高まっている。 
日銀が集計した2023年1月から 2026年8月までに公表された米国のデータセンター案件で
は、資金調達額は合計約1.6兆ドルに上る。このうち調達済みは約0.2兆ドルにとどまり、約1.4
兆ドルが計画段階にある。また、案件数の約2割でプライベートファンドがスポンサーとなって
いる（注2）。 
さらに、AI関連企業の間では、出資と将来の売上高が結びついた循環的な取引もみられる。IMF
が2026年10月に公表したGFSRによると、大手IT企業などが財務基盤の相対的に弱いAI企業に出
資し、出資を受けた企業が出資元などにサービスや半導体を発注する事例がある。IMFが確認した
2023年以降の14案件では、約3,000億ドルの出資と約6,500億ドルの債務調達が、約1.4兆ドル
の将来の売上高の約束と結びついている。出資先の経営が悪化すれば、将来の売上高と投融資の
価値が同時に損なわれる可能性がある。 
こうした資金調達の拡大によって、AI関連投資と銀行、プライベートファンドなどの結びつき
が強まっている。AI関連投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、データセンター事業者への融
資や出資を通じて、銀行やファンドに損失が生じる可能性がある。さらに、銀行はファンド自体
にも融資しており、ファンドの財務状況の悪化が銀行に波及する経路もある。日銀は、こうした
金融主体間の結びつきを通じて、ショックが金融システムに広がる可能性を警戒している。

3. 日本に及ぶ三つの波及経路 
AI関連投資の収益性に対する懸念の高まりや投資そのものの減速に直面した場合、日本の金融
システムにはどのような影響が及ぶのか。日銀の分析をもとに、①銀行融資、②金融市場、③実
体経済――の三つの波及経路からみていく。

（1） 銀行の融資を通じた経路 
日本の大手行は海外でデータセンター事業者やプライベートファンドへの融資を増やしてい
る。日銀によると、2026年 3月末時点で3メガ行の海外貸出全体に占めるデータセンター向けの
割合は 3％、海外ファンド向けは9％だった。また、3メガ行の海外ファンド向け貸出残高は12兆
円と、前年同月末から29.2％増加した。海外貸出全体に占める割合は現時点で限定的だが、収益
性の高さなどから注力分野となっている。データセンター事業者には賃料収入を特定の巨大IT企
業に依存するケースがあり、AI投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、融資の返済能力にも影
響しかねない。また、技術革新によって高性能半導体などの設備が短期間で陳腐化するリスクも
ある。 
海外ファンド向け融資も注視すべきだろう。銀行はファンドに直接融資しているほか、ファン
ドが投資する企業や事業にも資金を供給している。ファンド向け貸出は、信用力の高い投資家の
出資約束を担保とする形式が多く、投資適格の比率は高い。ただ、日銀は、海外ファンドの運用
実態や資産価値を適時に把握するのは必ずしも容易ではないと指摘する。市場で大きなショック
が生じれば、ファンドが契約済みの融資枠から資金を引き出し、銀行の資金供給負担が増える可

### Page 4

4/8 
能性もある。

（2） 金融市場を通じた経路 
日本株の上昇は、AI関連需要の恩恵が相対的に大きい電機、精密、非鉄、機械などの業種がけ
ん引してきた。日銀は、AI関連投資に見合った収益拡大が実現しない場合、日本株にも調整圧力
が生じ得ると指摘しており、相応の株式を保有する金融機関にとってもリスクとなる。 
さらに、日銀によると、世界の株式市場に投資するヘッジファンドでは、AI関連銘柄の保有割
合が高まっている。マクロ戦略を採るヘッジファンドでは、個別株式の保有額に占める米大手ハ
イテク 7社の割合が、2019年の約14％から2026年には約23％に上昇した（注3） 。こうしたファ
ンドのなかには、借り入れやデリバティブを活用して投資規模を拡大し、株式だけでなく国債な
どにも投資するものがある。AI関連株が急落すれば、損失の拡大や追加の証拠金請求などを受け
て、株式以外の投資ポジションも縮小する可能性がある。 
この影響が及び得る市場の一つが、日本の国債市場だ。ヘッジファンドを含む海外投資家は、
日本国債の現物と先物、金利スワップなどの価格差を利用した裁定取引を拡大させているとみら
れる。日銀によると、国債現物の対顧客取引に占める海外投資家の割合は、2015年の約3割から
2025年には6割強に上昇した（注4）。 
AI関連株の下落をきっかけにファンドがレバレッジを縮小すれば、国債関連のポジション解消
が進み、債券価格や金利の変動につながる可能性がある。日銀は、海外投資家による取引の増加
が市場の流動性や価格形成に寄与する場合がある一方、海外市場のショックが証拠金の請求など
をきっかけにポジション解消を引き起こし、日本の金融市場に影響を与えやすくなっている可能
性を指摘している。

（3） 実体経済を通じた経路 
日銀はストレステストで、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資が失速した場合、AI関連産業
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定している。日本でも、情報通信機械、はん用・
生産用機械、電気機械などのAI関連産業を中心に経済が減速する。企業収益の悪化は、アジア向
けを中心とする貸出の信用コスト増加を通じて、金融機関に跳ね返る。 
AI関連投資を巡る三つの波及経路は相互に関連している。設備投資の減速による企業収益の悪
化は、株価の下落や信用リスクの上昇にもつながり得る。日本と海外NBFIの結びつきは、すでに
相当な規模に達している。日本の銀行による海外NBFIへの投融資残高は、2008年 3月末の8兆円
から 2026年6月末には126兆円に増加した。海外の投資ファンド等による日本の株式・債券への
投資残高も、同期間に111兆円から408兆円へ拡大している（資料2）。このうち株式が355兆円
を占め、債券は53兆円にとどまる（注5） 。いずれもAI関連に限った金額ではないが、海外の金融
市場で発生したショックが日本に波及する経路の広がりを示している。

### Page 5

5/8

資料 2 日本の金融部門と海外ノンバンクの結びつき

（注）左図は国内銀行等による海外ノンバンクへの投融資残高。右図は、日銀がIMFの部門別対外証券投資データなどを用いて
試算した、海外主体による日本向け証券投資残高のうち「投資ファンド等」の値で、ディーラー・ブローカーやファイナンス会
社などを含む。海外ノンバンク全体の日本向け証券投資残高は 507兆円（2026年 6月末）。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-2（原資料はBIS、IMF、U.S. Department of the Treasury、
日本銀行）をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

4. 複合ショックにも耐性を示す日本の金融機関 
前章で整理した銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つの波及経路に沿って、日本の金融機関へ
の影響をみていこう。日銀は銀行105行、信用金庫247庫を対象に、2028年度末までの財務状況
を試算するマクロ・ストレステストを実施した。本稿で確認する「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」
では、AI関連投資の収益性に対する期待の縮小に加え、内外のNBFIによる資産売却などを通じて
金融市場の混乱が増幅される状況を想定した。 
まず、銀行融資を通じた波及経路を確認する。海外のデータセンター事業者やプライベートク
レジット（PC）ファンド向けの貸出が傷むというシナリオを置いた。両者に対する貸出の信用コ
スト率が最大約10ポイント悪化する想定だ。もっとも、国際統一基準行の信用コスト増加に占め
る海外ファンド・データセンター向けの寄与は限定的だった。 
金融市場を通じた経路では、株価や債券価格の大幅な下落を想定した。米国ではAI関連銘柄に
ITバブル崩壊後の下落率を当てはめるなどして、米国株価がベースラインから最大約50％下落す
るとした。日本株も最大約40％の下落を見込む。また、NBFIによるショックの増幅を織り込み、
米国のBBB格社債の信用スプレッドは最大約1,100ベーシスポイント拡大する。日米の長期金利も
ベースラインより1.5ポイント上昇する。こうした株価と債券価格の同時下落によって、金融機
関の有価証券評価損益などが悪化する。また、海外金利の上昇に伴う外貨調達コストの増加が、
海外の資金利益を押し下げる。 
実体経済を通じた経路では、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資の失速によって、関連産業

### Page 6

6/8 
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定した。日銀の試算では、アジアの実質GDPはベー
スラインから最大約7％下振れする。日本でも関連製造業を中心に企業収益が悪化し、銀行の信用
コスト増加につながる。今回のシナリオには原油高などの影響も含まれており、信用コストの増
加には国内向け貸出の寄与が大きい。 
こうした複合的なショックを想定しても、日銀の試算では金融機関の自己資本比率は全体とし
て規制水準を上回った。2028年度末の国際統一基準行の自己資本比率は、ベースラインの12.8％
から 8.9％に低下する（資料3）。

資料 3 マクロ・ストレステストにおける 2028年度末の自己資本比率

（注）対象は銀行 105行と信用金庫 247庫。「金融調整シナリオ」は、内外の金融市場や実体経済にリーマンショック並みの大幅
な調整が生じる想定。「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」は、内外金利の上昇、原油価格の上昇、AI関連投資の収益性に対する
期待の縮小などが同時に生じ、ノンバンク部門によるショックの増幅も織り込んだ想定。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅵ-2-5、Ⅵ-2-7をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

この水準はリーマンショック並みの市場調整を想定した「金融調整シナリオ」の10.1％を下回
る。今回のシナリオの影響の大きさが分かる。一方、国内基準行の銀行は10.4％から8.8％、信
用金庫は14.0％から13.2％への低下にとどまった。国内基準行では、金融調整シナリオ（銀行
8.6％、信用金庫12.6％）の方が低下幅は大きい。 
日銀は、AI関連投資への期待の縮小などがNBFIを通じて増幅される複合的なショックに対して
も、「金融システムの安定性は全体として維持される」と評価している。ただ、試算結果は業態ご
との平均値で、個々の金融機関の自己資本比率にはばらつきがある点には留意が必要だろう。

5. おわりに 
日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小やNBFIを通じたショックの増幅を織り
込んでも、日本の金融機関は全体として相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関

### Page 7

7/8 
連企業間の循環的な取引が、損失を増幅させる可能性にも注目したい。 
現在のAI関連企業の資金調達の特徴として、資金と売上が企業間で相互に結びつく関係があげ
られる。この循環的な取引を拙稿「AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書
から読み解く新たなリスク～」で指摘した。本稿の2章でも述べた通り、大手企業が財務基盤の
相対的に弱いAI企業に出資し、出資先が見返りとして大手企業のサービスや半導体を将来購入す
ることを約束する仕組みだ。IMFのGFSRによると、約6,500億ドルの債務のうち約3,200億ドルは
出資先が負っており、そこには出資元の保証付きの債務やオフバランスの債務も含まれる。出資
先の経営が悪化すれば、出資元の将来の売上高が減少すると同時に、出資や融資の価値も損なわ
れる可能性がある。IMFはこれを「誤方向リスク（wrong-way risk） 」と呼び、循環的な取引が損
失を増幅するリスクを評価するよう監督当局に求めている。 
筆者は、日本の金融機関もこの構造と無縁ではないと考える。データセンター事業者は、ハイ
パースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付けに、銀行などから資金を調達している。IMFによる
と、循環的な取引の出資を主導しているのはハイパースケーラーだ。出資先のAI企業の経営が悪
化すれば、取引相手となるハイパースケーラーの将来の売上高が下振れし、データセンターの需
要見通しにも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。そうなれば、ハイパースケーラーとのリース契約を裏付
けとする日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資も、間接的に影響を受ける可能性がある。表面
上は信用力の高いハイパースケーラーとの契約に見えても、その先に循環的な取引が組み込まれ
ていれば、リスクは見かけより高いのではないか。 
日本の金融機関のデータセンター向けやファンド向けの貸出は、現時点では海外貸出の一部に
とどまる。それでも、損失が特定の企業群にとどまらず、連鎖的に広がっていくリスクには目を
配っておきたい。AI関連投資が持続的な収益を生み出せるかに加え、企業間の出資と取引の結び
つきが、銀行やNBFIの資金供給を通じて金融システムにどう波及するのか。その把握には、個々
の融資先の信用力だけでなく、取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要になるだろう。

以 上

米国の巨大IT企業5社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracle）。 
2) 日銀「金融システムレポート」 （2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-9をもとに筆者が概算。対象は

まない。

高はディーラー対顧客取引分（顧客別にみた購入額と売却額の合計）で、国庫短期証券は含ま

Flex 
(FLEX.O/ FLEX US)

預期資料中心電源需求為獲利加速成長之主要動能 
重要訊息 
Flex FY2Q27 財報與 FY3Q27 財測均優於市場共識 ，公司上修 FY2027 資料
中心年增率自 70%至 80%，收盤股價上漲約 12%。 
評論及分析  
FY2Q27 資料中心電源需求升溫， EPS 年增 49%至 1.18 美元並優於共識。
Flex FY2Q27 (2026 年 7–9 月)營收年增 25%、季增 7%至 84.9 億美元，主
要來自資料中心電源 需求延續 ；受惠於電源產品營利率 年增 5.5ppt s 至約
16.0%、帶動產品組合轉佳， Flex FY2Q27  EPS 年增 49%、季增 18%至
1.18 美元，大致符合凱基原預估、優於市場共識 12%。 
FY3Q27 資料中心成長性可望優於 FY2Q27，預估 EPS 年增 52%至 1.32 美
元。Flex 已於 10 月完成對 EPC Power 的收購，該公司可望貢獻單季營收約
2 億美元，並有助於 800V 架構及固態變壓器( SST)的技術能力。FY 3Q27 隨
Hyperscalers 與 Neo clouds 等客戶需求延續，凱基預估 Flex 營收年增
33%、季增 11%至 93.9 億美元，其中資料中心業務之營收年增率預期達
115%，其餘產品線則約 10%上下；受惠營利率較佳的資料中心業務貢獻提
升，FY3Q27  EPS 預估年增 52%、季增 11%至 1.32 美元。 
凱基預估 Flex FY2027/FY2028  EPS 為 5.24/8.80 美元。基於電源產品接單
成長性優於預期以及併購 EPC Power 也將挹注營收，Flex 上修 FY2027 資料
中心營收年增率 自 70%至 80%，FY2028 則維持年增 80%之展望，隱含
FY2027/FY2028 資料中心營收分別上修 約 6% 、營收占比預期達
33%/45%。基於 Flex 上修展望，凱基 將 Flex FY2027/ FY2028 EPS 自原預估
之 4.96/7.96 美元上修至 5.24/8.80 美元，分別年增 59%/68%。 
投資建議  
凱基維持「增加持股」評等，目標價上修至 176 美元 (20x FY2028F EPS) 。 
投資風險  
關稅戰惡化導致客戶下單意願降低； CPI 業務營運成長態勢不如預期。

### Page 2

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

Flex 資料中心營收占比提升至 30%。Flex 三大產品線為 ITS (包含網通與智慧
生活，FY 2Q27 營收占比 39%；營利率 5.5%)、RMS ( 包含工業、汽車與醫
療，FY1Q27 營收占比 31%；營利率 6.4%)、CPI ( 資料中心業務，FY1Q27
營收占比 30%；營利率 10.7%)。各項業務之應用主要包含:  (1) 工業產品涵
蓋資本設備、工業裝置、再生能源、電源與電網邊緣等解決方案； (2) 車用
產品主要提供下一代移動方案、自駕、連網化 、電動化與智慧科技 ；(3) 健
康解決方案包含醫療裝置、醫療設備與藥物輸送系統； (4) 智慧生活包含消
費性電子、家電、消費性包裝、掃地機器人、微型移動裝置及音訊產品； (5) 
網通領域涵蓋通訊基礎設施與企業網路傳輸； (6) 資料中心業務 包含電源、
散熱、系統整合等產品與服務。   
Flex 目前的發展策略是結合 EMS 與產品的服務策略以提升接單能力，公司
表示自身是全球唯一具有提供從資料中心晶片到電網能力的供應商，除了可
提供嵌入式電源 (embedded power) 產品、管理伺服器 GPU 的電源，亦可
滿足客戶對資料中心內所有設施的電力與散熱需求，提供完整的客製化服
務，帶動公司資料中心營收占比自前一季之 28%提升至 FY2Q27 之 30%。

Flex FY2027/ FY2028 資料中心營收與獲利成長性可望優於產業平均 。Flex
計劃將 CPI 部門分拆成一家新的上市公司 Axiom，維持在 CY1Q27 完成分拆
的進度不變， Flex 自身將專注於醫療保健、機器人、自動化倉儲和網通等產
業，兩家公司可更聚焦 於彼此的業務並提升資金運用效率。公司 提及自身在
資料中心電源布局相對完整，且產品價格亦相對具有競爭優勢下，使公司在
電源產品接單成長性優於產業平均； 隨包含 Google 在內的 Hyperscalers 、
Colocations 及 Neo-clouds 等客戶對嵌入式電源與 系統級解決方案的需求升
溫，公司在此次會議將 FY2027 資料中心營收年增率自 70%上修至 80%、
FY2028 則維持年增 80%之展望，皆較 FY2026 資料中心業務營收年增 38%
至 66 億美元加速成長 ，並隱含 FY2027/FY2028 資料中心營收分別上 修約
6%、營收占比預期達 33%/45%。

### Page 3

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  3

獲利性部分， 受惠於電源產品營利率較佳、 FY2Q27 年增 5.5ppts 至約
16.0%，優於資料中心業務的平均營利率約 11%以及公司整體平均營利率
約 7%，公司上修 FY2027 資料中心業務之營利率約 1ppt 至 11.6%。凱基
預估隨資料中心營收貢獻提升， FY2027/FY2028 營 利占比分別達
49%/65%，為公司主要獲利動能。  
至於資料中心以外之 其餘應用，預期營收年增率 維持約 10%上下之水準。
目前歐洲工業需求略 有回溫，營收年增率可望維持約 10%上下；汽車產品
線則因目前全球汽車銷售量已逐步回升至過往 9,000 萬輛的正常水準，
FY2027 /FY2028 產品線成長性預期落在 5%以內；醫療設備則因客戶先前在
Covid -19 時期堆積的庫存水位過高，目前庫存去化雖逐步落底，但 需求端仍
相對平緩；網通領域則預期隨庫存去化力道放緩， FY2027 產品線營收將逐
步回溫，預期年增率 約 10%；消費性電子則因記憶體漲價、品牌廠將成本
轉嫁給消費者的影響， FY202 7 營收預期年減約 5-10%。 
Flex 評等維持「增加持股」，目標價上修至 176 美元。基於 Flex 上修資料
中心之營收與營 利率展望，凱基將 Flex FY2027/FY2028 EPS 自原預估之
4.96/7.96 美元上修至 5.24/8.80 美元，分別年增 59%/68%；相較市場共識
FY202 7/FY2028  EPS 預估為 4.48/6.82 美元，股價交易於約 12/14 倍之
FY2028 凱基與市場共識 EPS 預估。 
Flex 過往五年本益比多落於 5-20 倍之間，考量 (1) 公司在資料中心領域掌握
從晶片、液冷散熱、BBU 到電網的供應能力 ，布局相對多數同業更全面 ；(2) 
公司持續提升在 CSP 廠供應鏈之接單量，有助 FY2027 /FY2028 營收與獲利
加速成長； (3) 基於資料中心產品線營利率可望逐步提升至 10-15%、顯著
優於公司過往產品之 5%水準，且在獲利占比逐步提升至 50%以上的帶動
下，有助公司評價向散熱與電源同業靠攏。綜合上述 原因，評估 Flex 本益比
可望回升至區間 上緣之 20 倍上下浮動 ；基於 Flex 當前評價偏低 ，故維持
「增加持股」之投資評等，目標價隨 EPS 預估上修而調整至 176 美元 (20x 
FY202 8F EPS)。

### Page 7

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 7

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

Document 313: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital 
30-year government bond benchmark yields (%) 
          













### Page 2

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

reflected in other regions too. Today, a 2-standard deviation move in 10-year Treasury 
yields would equate to about 50bps over a month or 30bps over two weeks, similar to 
the adjustments in recent days which explains the pullback in equity prices.

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

In the US, in particular, the forward PE multiple for the S&P has come down from 22x at

### Page 4

the start of the year to 19x, in line with its long-run average, despite the market being 
close to its all-time high. 
5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

The combination of higher cost of capital and greater capital intensity in the tech sector 
has reduced the value of their future cash flows and triggered a de-rating in the biggest 
companies.  The dominant capex hyperscalers in the US now have a forward PE close to 
the average of the rest of the market.

8. Software companies have also de-rated on the back of fears of disruption from AI 
while memory and chips stocks have also experienced lower multiples as their earnings

### Page 7

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

Stock correlations are at multi-year lows across the major equity markets (Exhibit 12).

Even within sectors like technology, correlations have fallen sharply (Exhibit 13). The 
‘magnificent seven’ that was long seen as a homogeneous group of technology winners 
has also seen significant dispersion of returns. Differentiation, diversification and alpha 
generation are likely to remain the key drivers of equity returns. With higher bond yields, 
valuations are not likely to rise making earnings the critical driver of returns for investors.

### Page 1

The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to result in a material
increase in demand for bank bonds. Securitisations beneﬁt
most directly through lower capital charges, while covered
bonds could beneﬁt modestly from the new liquidity-buﬀer
rules
Maureen Schuller
On 30 January 2027, the amendments to the Solvency II regime
will apply, making these revisions one of the important regulatory
themes for 2027. One of the key objectives of the Solvency II

reforms is to release capital and promote long-term investments by
European insurance companies in support of the ambitions of
Europe’s Savings and Investments Union (SIU). Such capital release
could also increase the capacity of insurance companies to invest
more in corporate and bank bonds.
During the past decade, insurance companies have reduced their
bond holdings in favour of equity. Rather than an active
reallocation of assets, this partially reﬂects second-round eﬀects

related to the strong equity market growth and the rise in bond

yields and corresponding decline in bond valuations against the
backdrop of the ECB rate hikes in 2022 and 2023. Higher yields
have also coincided in recent years with a stronger focus by banks
on issuance in shorter maturities, rather than the longer maturities
traditionally favoured by insurance companies. This has been
particularly noteworthy for covered bonds.

Source: EIOPA, ING
We don’t expect the Solvency II revisions to reverse this trend,
particularly as the amendments promote long-term equity
investments. Instead, for most bank bonds, the spread-risk capital
treatment remains broadly unchanged. Unsecured and
subordinated bank bonds continue to be treated as corporate

bonds with capital charges determined by their ratings and
duration, while qualifying covered bonds keep their preferential
treatment. The impact of the Solvency II revisions therefore arises
mainly indirectly through lower overall capital requirements, while
securitisations are the only debt instruments beneﬁting directly
through a more favourable capital treatment.
Nonetheless, certain reforms are worth highlighting for their
potential impact on insurance demand for diﬀerent bank bonds.

Among these, the amendments relating to long-term equity

### Page 3

investments, securitisations and the volatility adjustment are
particularly noteworthy.
Long-term equity investments and non-
life liquidity buffers
Under Solvency II, insurance companies can classify part of their
equity holdings as long-term equity investments (LTEI), beneﬁting
from a lower 22% capital charge. To qualify, these investments
must be held for at least ﬁve years, be listed in the EEA or OECD,
meet diversiﬁcation standards and be managed under a long-term

investment policy. The insurer must also be able to avoid forced
sales for ﬁve years, even under stressed conditions.
The Solvency II amended Delegated Regulation introduces two
approaches to demonstrate that forced equity sales can be
avoided. 1) The insurer must either have suﬃcient illiquid
homogeneous life insurance liabilities with a duration of more than
9.5 years or a liquidity buﬀer exceeding 100% for non-life insurance
obligations. 2) The insurer can also apply a forced-selling test to

prove that it can generate cash inﬂows that are consistently higher
than the cash outﬂows over ﬁve years, including under stress.
The liquidity buﬀer requirements for non-life insurance activities
are similar to the Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR) requirements for
banks. The portfolio of liquid assets can comprise Level 1, Level 2A
and Level 2B assets, with the total value of Level 2A and Level 2B
assets capped at 40% and the value of the Level 2B assets at 15%.

Qualifying Level 1 assets, subject to a 0% haircut, are cash,
exposures to the ECB, multilateral development banks and
international organisations, and domestic currency exposures
to EEA central governments and central banks.
Qualifying Level 2A assets, subject to a 15% haircut, are bonds
and loans rated AA- or better that are not issued by an
insurance company or bank, and covered bonds rated AA- or
better as referred to in Article 3(1) of the European Covered

Bond Directive (CBD) (ie ‘European Covered Bonds’).
Qualifying Level 2B assets, subject to a 25% haircut, are
Simple, Transparent and Standardised (STS) securitisations (not

While unsecured bank bonds are excluded as eligible liquid assets,

the LTEI-related liquidity buﬀer approach could generate some
additional demand for covered bonds or STS securitisations of
banks. However, we don’t expect this eﬀect to be signiﬁcant given
the relatively small share of non-life insurance obligations and
insurers’ sizeable existing portfolios of other liquid assets, such as
government bonds.

The LTEI liquidity approach is separate from the generic liquidity
and concentration risk management requirements under Solvency
II, as well as from the obligation under the amended Solvency II
Directive to provide for a liquidity risk management plan.
Lower capital requirements for
securitisations

### Page 5

The amended Solvency II Delegated Regulation lowers the capital
requirements for securitisation notes. This should encourage
insurers to invest more in these transactions, helping banks to
transfer risk and expand lending.
The risk factors (a and b ) for the spread risk of senior tranches
of STS securitisations will become fully aligned with the
treatment of similarly rated covered bonds as referred to in CBD
Article 3(1).
The risk factors for non-senior STS securitisations are reduced

to a similar extent, with the new capital charges for AAA-rated
non-senior notes at c.70% of the current capital charge, and for
AA-rated non-senior notes at 75% of the current requirement.
The diﬀerence in the capital treatment between STS and non-
STS securitisations is currently far more signiﬁcant for insurers
than for banks. This diﬀerence will be reduced substantially,
while the capital charge remains capped at 100%.
The current Solvency II regime makes no distinction between

senior and non-senior non-STS securitisation notes,
exaggerating the spread risk of senior compared with non-
senior tranches. Hence, lower risk factors are introduced for
senior non-STS compared to non-senior non-STS transactions.
As a result of these amendments, the diﬀerence in capital
requirements between CBD-compliant covered bonds and senior
STS securitisations will disappear from the end of January, while in
the highest rating buckets (AAA and AA) securitisations will be

treated better than unsecured bonds or third-country covered
bonds. Further down the rating scale, STS securitisations will be
treated closer to that of unsecured bonds from a capital
perspective, where corporate and bank unsecured bonds will still
beneﬁt from a more favourable capital charge than similarly rated
senior STS securitisations.
Securitisation alternatives will become
more competitive from a capital perspective

### Page 6

Source: European Commission, ING
European insurance companies currently hold only about €37bn in
collateralised debt, such as asset-backed securities (ABS) and
residential mortgage-backed securities (RMBS). This is only 12% of
what they hold in covered bonds and 3% of their corporate debt
portfolio.
The Solvency II amendments may motivate insurance companies
to diversify their debt portfolios a bit further away from covered
bonds and other bank bond products towards securitisations.

However, the impact is not expected to be substantial given that
the weighted average lives of securitisation notes may not always
be well suited to the longer-term investment needs of insurance
companies.
Volatility adjustment to align spread
sensitivities more closely
Insurance companies calculate the best estimate of their liabilities
by discounting the expected future liability cash ﬂows using the
relevant risk-free interest rate term structure. To mitigate the

balance sheet impact of bond spread volatility (ie, on the asset side
of their balance sheet), insurance companies can discount their
liabilities at the risk-free rate plus a volatility adjustment (VA).
The volatility adjustment increases the
discount rate

### Page 7

Spot rate, %
Source: EIOPA, ING
For each relevant currency (or national market in the case of the
euro area), the volatility adjustment represents 65% of the spread
(S) between the interest rate on the reference portfolio and the
relevant risk-free interest rate, adjusted for a risk correction (RC). The
latter correction factor removes the part of the spread that is
attributable to expected losses or unexpected credit or other risks
to the assets. The volatility adjustment is only added to the

applicable risk-free interest rates that are not derived from
extrapolation.
For each relevant country, a country-speciﬁc adjustment may also
be added to the volatility adjustment before the application of the
65% factor if a) the risk-corrected country spread is more than twice
the risk-corrected currency spread, and b) the risk-corrected country
spread is more than 85bp. The country component in the volatility
adjustment helps mitigate excessive widening in speciﬁc countries.

Under the current regime, the representative currency and country
portfolios are constructed as uniform portfolios, even though
insurers’ actual ﬁxed-income portfolios may diﬀer substantially.
Some insurers can therefore beneﬁt excessively from the volatility
adjustment if the resulting reduction in the best estimate of their
liabilities exceeds the decline in the value of the relevant assets
when credit spreads widen.

### Page 8

For this reason, the Solvency II revisions change the calculation of
the volatility adjustment by a) making the applicable risk
corrections (RC) more spread-sensitive, and b) introducing a credit
spread sensitivity ratio (CSSR) to better align the volatility
adjustment with the insurer’s asset-liability proﬁle. The latter ratio
reﬂects the price value of a basis point of the insurer’s bond,
securitisation and loan investments versus the price value of a

basis point of the best estimate of liabilities, with a minimum of 0
and a maximum of 1.
Moreover, subject to approval by the supervisory authority, an
undertaking-speciﬁc adjustment (USA) of up to 105% may be
applied to align the risk-corrected spread on the reference portfolio
more closely with that of the insurer’s own portfolio. This must not
lead to an increase in the applied risk-adjusted spread for more
than two consecutive quarters. Moreover, when the USA is used, the

insurer cannot also increase the volatility adjustment through a
macro volatility adjustment.
The macro volatility adjustment replaces the existing country-
speciﬁc adjustment and can be added to the euro volatility
adjustment when the relevant conditions are met. It aims to
mitigate the impact of excessive spread widening in individual euro
area countries and is based on the diﬀerence between the risk-
corrected euro spread and the risk-corrected spread for the relevant

country.
Volatility adjustment calculations

Subscripts cu = currency, co = country, ma = macro; VA = volatility
adjustment, S = spread of the reference portfolio, RC = risk correction, RCS
= risk-corrected spread of the reference portfolio, CSSR = credit spread
sensitivity ratio, USA = undertaking-speciﬁc adjustment.
Source: European Commission, ING
The introduction of additional minimum conditions, such as
supervisory approval and the availability of adequate processes to
calculate the volatility adjustment, has also facilitated the upward

revision of the general application ratio (GAR) from 65% to 85%.
Primarily through the introduction of the credit spread sensitivity
ratio (CSSR), the volatility adjustment will better reﬂect the
relationship between each insurer’s asset and liability sensitivity to
changes in the volatility adjustment. The revision may provide
modest support for bonds that increase the asset portfolio’s spread
sensitivity, as a higher CSSR results in a larger volatility adjustment

until the ratio reaches its cap of one. The revised calculations should
also reduce the possibility for some insurers to beneﬁt
disproportionately from the volatility adjustment when their assets
are less spread-sensitive than the reference portfolio.
Conclusion
The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to trigger a material
reallocation in favour of bank bonds. Securitisations are the most
obvious beneﬁciaries of a more favourable capital treatment, while

covered bonds and unsecured bank bonds should mainly beneﬁt
indirectly from lower overall capital requirements for insurers. We

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
Fiji’s resilience is being tested...
Fiji’s economy entered 2026 with growth already normalising
from the post-pandemic recovery. Real GDP growth slowed from
3.6% in 2024 to 2.5% in 2025. Foreign reserves started the year

around $3.7bn, banking-system liquidity remained high and the
RBF maintained its Overnight Policy Rate at 0.25%.
Activity so far into 2026 has been somewhat stronger than what
we feared. Visitor arrivals reached 762,352 during January–
September, up 3.7%yr, while construction, investment lending,
cement sales, remittances and private-sector credit remained
firm. Nevertheless, Westpac Fiji has kept its 2026 growth
forecast unchanged at 2.0%. Stronger headline indicators are

not translating one-for-one into real economic growth because
higher prices and intermediate costs are absorbing a growing
share of nominal activity.
The outlook changed materially in March as the Middle East
conflict disrupted global energy markets. Fiji remains heavily
dependent on imported petroleum products, so higher refined-
fuel, freight and insurance costs flowed through transport,
electricity, food distribution and wider business expenses. By

June, motor spirit price reached $3.93/litre and diesel at a record
$4.58/litre, while automotive-fuel sales value rose 24.4%yr in the
June quarter.
As this report goes out, the Middle East conflict remains a
key external downside risk. At least 12 vessels were attacked
in the week ending 16 October and yet it is reported that oil
flows through Hormuz averaged 11.6mn barrels per day to 13
October, about 68% of pre-war levels through alternative and

costly routes. Iran says a seven-day reopening proposal remains
available, but US-Iran negotiations remain unresolved.
Westpac Economics’ most recent scenario analysis “ Persistent
energy pressures have further to run ” indicates that relief will
be gradual. Brent crude is forecast to average US$101/bbl in Q4
2026 before easing through 2027. Refined products are expected
to normalise more slowly because refinery capacity, inventories,
freight and insurance remain constrained. Under Westpac’s

escalation scenario, in a return to open conflict Brent could reach
around US$150/bbl in H1 2027.
Domestic tourism sector demonstrates the nominal-versus-
real growth tension. June-quarter visitor arrivals increased to
287,588, yet earnings fell 4.9%yr to $708.1mn. First-half tourism
earnings declined 1.7%yr despite higher arrivals, even though
hotel takings increased 9.5%yr.
Australia and New Zealand remain critical, together contributing
around 71% of September arrivals. Westpac expects Australian

growth to slow from 2.1% in 2026 to 1.5% in 2027, while New
Zealand strengthens from 1.9% to 2.6%. Tourism therefore
increasingly needs to focus on yield rather than visitor numbers
alone.
Shamal Chand
Senior Economist, Westpac Pacific Economics

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
...but the buffers are still doing the heavy lifting...
There are good reasons why growth held up despite these shocks.
Construction work put in place reached $155.9mn in the June
quarter, up 8.6%yr, while investment lending, cement sales and

construction-related imports subsequently remained strong.
Industrial production increased 1.5%yr, although performance
remained uneven across industries.
Inward personal remittances have been particularly impressive,
reaching $1.2bn during January–August, up 32.7%yr. We expect
remittances to reach $1.55bn this year. These inflows, with strong
diaspora support, are helping households absorb higher living
costs while simultaneously supporting Fiji’s external position.

However, the external accounts show the cost of this resilience.
Fiji’s merchandise trade deficit widened to $2.9bn in the first half,
with imports reaching $4.3bn against exports of $1.4bn. Mineral-
fuel imports alone reached around $1.2bn, up 57%yr. Foreign
reserves nevertheless recovered to approximately $4.09bn by
end-September, equivalent to 5.7 months of retained imports , also
supported by Government’s external loan draw-down.
Our in-house import-demand indicator is signalling softer

domestic momentum. Import activity over the three months
to September was down 21.3%yr and fell 39.8% compared
with the preceding three months. The broader six-month
measure declined at a more moderate 16.1% annualised pace.
While volatile, the trend adds to our view that higher prices are
weighing on real activity, despite continued strength across
tourism, remittances, credit and construction.
Headline inflation reached 7.6%yr in August before easing to

6.8% in September, with transport prices up 12.2%yr and food
prices up 7.6%. Our latest forecast has November inflation
around 8.0%yr, average inflation around 4.3% for 2026 and
December (year-end) inflation at 7.4%.
Higher fuel prices are no longer affecting only households.
The shock is moving through freight, electricity, agriculture,
construction materials and wider supply chains. For instance,
we note recent increases in taxi and bus fares. These higher

intermediate costs compress business margins and reduce
household purchasing power.
This also informs our 2027 view. Westpac Fiji has downgraded
its 2027 growth forecast to 2.5%, reflecting the prevailing
effects of the energy crisis, elevated refined-fuel prices and the
broader transmission of higher intermediate costs into next year.
Westpac’s global outlook supports this caution: crude prices are
expected to ease gradually, but refined products could remain

comparatively expensive for longer.
Renewable energy is increasingly important because the fuel
crisis has put a clear economic price on Fiji’s imported-energy
dependence. The faster Fiji prioritises a shift to renewable energy,
the sooner it can establish a more stable energy foundation.
El Niño creates an additional risk through agriculture, hydro
generation and food prices. The buffers remain meaningful, but
they are being asked to work significantly harder.

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
...as risks converge
Government finances add another dimension to the outlook.
The FY2025/26 deficit came in at $597.9mn, or 4.3% of GDP,
substantially better than originally budgeted.
Nevertheless, financing requirements are substantial.

Government plans to raise $1.48bn in FY2026/27, comprising
$1.046bn in deficit financing and $436.3mn refinancing. Around
$853.9mn to be domestically sourced and $627.95mn externally,
including a $444mn ADB policy-based loan in November.
Domestic yields therefore deserve closer attention. Fiji’s
government yield curve has steepened considerably, with the
adjustment concentrated beyond 10 years. While the 10-year
yield remained at 3.90%, the 20-year yield rose by 80 basis points

between July 2025 and September 2026 to 5.80%. The 25-year
yield increased even more sharply, climbing from 5.94% to 7.19%
after peaking at 7.25% in mid-2026.
Pressure is also emerging at the short end. The Government’s
20 October Treasury Bill tender offered $50mn across three-,
six- and 12-month maturities. Investors bid for the full amount,
but sought yields of 0.35%, 0.60% and 1.30%, respectively,
above the listed yields of 0.30%, 0.55% and 1.25%. Government

did not accept the higher yields, leaving the entire $50mn offer
unallocated. This suggests investors are increasingly seeking
better returns even on shorter-dated Government securities.
The financial system still provides considerable protection.
Banking-system liquidity stood around $2.28bn at end-
September and the RBF maintained its OPR at 0.25%. In August,
the weighted-average lending rate was 4.51%, the savings-
deposit rate 0.31% and the time-deposit rate 2.01%.

For now, high liquidity limits immediate crowding-out risks.
Higher government yields may encourage banks and institutional
investors to allocate more funds towards government securities.
If liquidity subsequently tightens, deposit rates may need to rise
to retain savings, adding pressure to bank funding costs and
eventually lending rates.
S&P’s decision to maintain Fiji’s B+/B ratings but revise the
outlook from stable to negative reinforces the importance of

debt sustainability, investor confidence and credible medium-
term fiscal consolidation.
Political uncertainty adds another layer, with ongoing public
debate on constitutional amendments and the general election
due by 6 February 2027.
This brings us back to the growth story. We have maintained
2026 growth at 2.0% because resilient tourism, remittances,
construction and investment are being offset by unexpectedly
high inflation and intermediate costs. Our downgrade of 2027

growth to 2.5% recognises that this adjustment will take longer
than previously expected, while fiscal, financial, energy, climate
and political risks increasingly overlap.

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
INFLATION OUTLOOK
Cost pressures move beyond the pump
Inflation has emerged as a key pressure point for Fiji, threatening 
to erode gains from otherwise resilient domestic activity. 
Headline inflation accelerated to 6.8%yr in September, driven by 
essentials: food prices rose 7.6%yr, transport increased 12.2%yr 
and housing and utilities were 4.9% higher. The underlying 
picture remains less severe, with prices declining 0.4%mth in 
September and annual-average inflation at 1.9%. Nevertheless, 
the acceleration suggests the global fuel shock is increasingly 
working its way through the domestic economy.

The import-price pipeline reinforces this view. Import prices 
increased 4.9%qtr and 4.4%yr in the June quarter, including 
a 15.4%yr rise in mineral-product prices as petrol, diesel and 
aviation-fuel costs increased. Export prices rose 6.2%yr, although 
the terms of trade eased 0.6%qtr. Elevated energy costs are 
also spreading beyond the pump. Transport-services producer 
prices increased 2.7%qtr in June, while building-material prices 
rose 1.5%qtr in September, signalling broader pressure across 
distribution, construction and services.

Fuel-price pressures intensified again in October, when regulated 
unleaded prices in Viti Levu increased to $3.15/litre and diesel 
rose by 21 cents to $3.41/litre. Prices nevertheless remain below 
their June peaks. Our indicative estimates point to further 
increases in fuel prices over the coming months.

The global outlook offers little immediate relief. Westpac 
Economics expects Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 2026 
before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027, but refined fuels are 
expected to ease more slowly as refinery supply remains more 
constrained, while shipping, insurance and inventory pressures 
gradually normalise. Westpac’s Australian forecasts provide 
some regional context, with diesel and petrol expected to 
average 274c/l and 230c/l, respectively, in Q4 before declining 
through 2027.

For Fiji, the distinction between crude and refined-product prices 
is important. Singapore gasoil prices, freight and insurance costs 
are expected to remain elevated and ease only gradually through 
2027, meaning lower Brent prices may not translate immediately 
into cheaper imported fuel. Higher diesel costs feed into freight, 
construction, agriculture and distribution, while aviation-
fuel pressures add to airline operating costs and tourism-
sector margins. Repeated shocks increase the likelihood that 
businesses eventually pass more of these costs on to consumers, 
particularly through food and other essentials.

We consequently expect inflation to remain elevated through 
year-end, rising towards 8.0% in November before easing to 7.4% 
in December, taking annual-average inflation to around 4.3%. 
The concern is increasingly that an imported fuel shock develops 
into broader cost-push inflation into next year. The squeeze on 
real purchasing power makes inflation an increasingly important 
downside risk to Fiji’s growth outlook heading into 2027.
Source: FBoS, Macrobond, Westpac Economics
